Core function `conv_coeffs(p, n)` -> c length 2n-1. Use `scipy.fft.next_fast_len`? We can precompute N = next_power_of_two(2n-1) or `next_fast_len` for faster (e.g. 65536 for n=32768). Use rfft? p real; A=rfft(p,N), c=irfft(A*A,N)[:2n-1]. This halves. Use scipy.fft.rfft/irfft.

Core function `objective(p)` = 2*n*max(c). If p sum not 1? Use p normalized.

Core function `gradient_weights(c, mode)`:
- If LSE: w_k = softmax(β c), normalized sum1 (so weights sum 1) or not? Gradient scale arbitrary; use sum1 for step. To avoid overflow, shift by max. If β large, weights concentrate near max. `w = exp(β*(c-cmax))/sum`. Then corr = 2*correlation(w,p). However if w sum=1, gradient norm scales. We can use step sizes accordingly.
- If top-k: choose top k lags, weights maybe c_k - median or just 1/k. To reduce noise, use exponential weights among top k: w_k = softmax(β_top c_k) for active. β_top maybe 10 to 100. Or use all with w=kexp(β c), which is LSE. LSE is smooth.

But LSE gradient may be too smooth for low beta; for high beta approximate max. Use adaptive β. For final polish, β high. But high β gradient with small amplitude; step size needs adjust.

Optimizers:

A) Projected subgradient in p-simplex:
- p normalized sum1. At each iteration:
  - c, M.
  - weights w (LSE or topk). grad = 2*corr(w,p). This is gradient of ∑ w_k c_k. For max, subgradient if w on active.
  - Need decrease ∑w c (smooth surrogate). Step α = 1/(L?); Since linear objective in p with gradient g. We can use line search: try p_try = project(p - α g, 1), evaluate surrogate or actual M. Use backtracking.
  - Projection onto simplex expensive but ok. Could use faster `project_simplex` in-place.
  - Adaptive α: α = step_scale / ||g||^2 * maybe (M - surrogate average)? Or use α = 2*gap/||g||^2 where gap=M-mean(c)? Previous. We can improve by using step = line search from `1.0/||g||` to `10.0/||g||`.
  - Since objective M, use actual accepted if cmax_try < M*(1- decisão). But to make progress on surrogate, accept if surrogate < current? We'll track best.
  - Add random perturbations/restarts when stuck.

B) Log-domain Adam with LSE:
- q = log(p + ε) maybe; p=softmax(q). Use objective LSE: f_β = (1/β) log ∑ exp(β c_k) (smooth). Gradient wrt q: p * grad_p. We want minimize f_β, not max. For β high, approximates max. Use Adam with step size maybe 1e-3 to 1e-2. Periodically increase β.
- This can run many iterations. Normalization automatic. Could get stuck due to softmax plateau. Use multiple β schedules.
- Alternatively, optimize p directly with away-steps? Log-domain avoids projection and may find endpoint zeros (p small). Good.
- Need compute gradient wrt q: grad_q = grad_p * p. If c computed, grad_p = 2*corr(w,p) for LSE weights w (weights of LSE). But if objective is LSE, w = softmax(β c); gradient of LSE wrt p is 2*corr(w,p). Then chain: grad_q = p * that. Since p sum1. Use Adam. Also can normalize q subtract mean to stabilize.
- Step size: Since f_β scale, gradient maybe small. Use adaptive: `lr = 1.0 / (beta * something)`? Let's derive. For LSE, gradient wrt p entries have units p. Sum w=1, grad_p ~2p. Adam per coordinate can handle; use `lr=0.01` maybe. But for high beta, gradient sharp; use line search? Could do Nesterov? Another method: Use iterative reweighting / Markov chain: Want p*p flat. We can set target M and update p_i ∝ 1 / ∑_{k active} λ_k p_{k-i}. This resembles KKT fixed point: For active lags K, define D_i = ∑_{k∈K, valid} p_{k-i}. Update p_i = 1 / D_i (renormalized) for active? If p_i>0 and KKT constant, p_i ≈ 1/D_i. This is like power iteration for inverse. Could be effective. Algorithm:
- Start p.
- Repeat:
   - c = conv(p), M = max c.
   - Choose active weights λ over lags near M (e.g exponential/cutoff).
   - Compute D_i = ∑_k λ_k p_{k-i}.
   - Update p_i = 1/(D_i + ε) maybe (or p_i^γ / D_i) and normalize; optionally mix with old p.
This is like solving T p = constant. Since T positive, inverse can be approximated by power iteration: p_new = T^{-1}1. Multiplicative update maybe. Could converge to KKT for active set. It automatically keeps positive. We can tune. This might be much better than gradient.

Derive from KKT: α = (T_λ p)_i. Thus p_i ∝ 1/(T_λ p)_i. We can estimate T_λ p by correlation of λ and p. For active λ, D_i=correlation(λ,p)_i. Use update p = 1/(D_i+ε). But if λ small, D smooth. Use λ from c: λ_k ∝ exp(β(c_k-M)) (LSE weights). Then D ≈ weighted average of p shifts. This is nonlinear. We can also do exponentiated gradient: p_i ← p_i * (α/D_i)^η. At KKT α/D_i=1.

Maybe combine with line search. This is similar to entropy scaling. Let's test intuition: For uniform p, c triangular, active λ peaks central. D_i near? It might push p to reduce high convolution. Could converge.

Alternatively use Sinkhorn-like: We want outer product p_i p_j to have constrained anti-diagonal sums. This is matrix scaling of a rank-1 matrix to meet row/antidiagonal sums. If target all c_k≤M, maybe find rank-1 matrix with anti-diagonal sums. Multiplicative updates: For each anti-diagonal k with sum c_k, scale factors on rows/columns to balance. Matrix M_{i,j}=p_i p_j. Constraints on anti-diagonals (i+j=k). To reduce high anti-diagonal k, reduce some p_i. A multiplicative update: For each active k, multiply p_i and p_j by factors? This is like iterative proportional fitting (IPF) for a 3-index tensor? Variables p_i appear in products p_i p_j. If we want set c_k=M, we can do p_i ← p_i / sqrt(D_i) as above.

We can include a "KKT fixed-point" optimizer:
```
for it:
 c = conv(p)
 w = softmax(beta*c)  # dual weights length L
 D = corr(w,p)  # T_w p
 # solve constant approx: alpha = median/mean of D over support? We want update p to make D constant: p_new_i ∝ 1/D_i^gamma
 D_pos = np.clip(D, eps, None)
 p_new = D_pos ** (-gamma)
 p = (1-t)*p + t*normalize(p_new)
```

But scipy fft rfft accepts n parameter; choose power of two for simplicity. For n=16384, L=32767, nearest fast 32768; for 32768,L=65535, fast 65536. Use power of two.

```
def _conv(p):
    n=len(p)
    L=2*n-1
    N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    P=rfft(p,N)
    c=irfft(P*P,N)[:L]
    np.maximum(c,0,out=c)
    return c
```
Note rfft of size N length N//2+1; P*P; irfft. For real p, ok. Could use double. Need avoid modifying p.

```
def _objective(p):
    n=len(p)
    c=_conv(p)
    return 2.0*n*c.max()
```

```
def _normalize(p, s=1.0):
    p=np.asarray(p,float)
    p=np.clip(p,0,None)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0: return np.ones(len(p))/len(p)
    return p*(s/p.sum())
```

```
def run_kkt_inverse(p, time_limit, beta, gamma=0.5):
   best...
   for it:
```

Detailed `run_log_optimizer(p0, time_limit, rng, beta_factor=None, lr=0.01, beta_min=None, beta_max=None, gamma=???)`:
```
def run_log_optimizer(p0, time_limit, rng, beta_init_factor=None, beta_stability=..., deterministic=False):
    p = _normalize(p0)
    n=len(p)
    L=2*n-1
    N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    bestM=objective...; bestp=p
    q=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); q-=q.mean()
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
    b1,b2,eps=0.9,0.999,1e-8
    t=1
    lr=0.01
    lr_max=0.05
    previousM=bestM
    no_improve=0
    beta = initial beta (e.g. 5*n)
    start=time.monotonic()
    verbose=True maybe print every 200s.
    while time.monotonic()-start < time_limit:
        # ensure p=softmax(q) consistent maybe after q update
        c=_conv_same_N(p,N)
        M=c.max()
        if M < bestM - 1e-15:
           bestM=M; bestp=p.copy(); no_improve=0
        else:
           no_improve +=1
        # update beta based on gap
        # Use quantile maybe median to avoid costly sort? np.median sort O(nlogn) on L~32k not huge but every iter too expensive. Use c.mean? Since sum c=1 (within error) mean=1/L. Gap to mean is easy: `gap = M - (1.0/L)`.
        mean_c=1.0/L
        gap = M - mean_c
        if gap <= 1e-15: beta=beta_max
        else: beta_target = 20.0 / gap
        beta = max(beta*1.0005, min(beta_max, beta_target))? We want increase gradually. Or `beta = min(beta_max, beta*1.001)`.
        # weights
        scaled=beta*(c - M) # M = cmax
        # clip to [-60,0]
        np.clip(scaled, -60.0, 0.0, out=scaled)
        w=np.exp(scaled)
        ws=w.sum()
        if ws==0: w[0]=1? Actually max index 0 of exp(0)=1; min ws≥1. 
        w/=ws
        gp=2.0*_corr_same_N(w,p,N)
        # exact log gradient
        gp_dot = float(np.dot(p, gp))
        gq = p * (gp - gp_dot)
        # Adam update
        t+=1
        m = b1*m + (1-b1)*gq
        v = b2*v + (1-b2)*(gq*gq)
        mhat = m/(1.0 - b1**t)  # can precompute? t large, b1**t underflow; 1. ok.
        vhat = v/(1.0 - b2**t)
        q -= lr * mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+eps)
        q -= q.mean() # center
        # optional small L2 regularization to avoid endpoint explosion? q *=? no.
        p_new = _softmax(q)
        # evaluate new M? We'll evaluate next loop, but for adaptive lr maybe compute now? That doubles conv. Instead next loop.
        p=p_new
        # Track rough divergence: if M from previous? We'll check at top next.
        if no_improve > 1000:
            # inject noise in q
            noise_scale = 0.02 * max(1.0, lr)
            q = q + rng.normal(0, noise_scale, n)
            q -= q.mean()
            no_improve=0
            lr = max(0.001, lr*0.5)
        if it%1000==0 print.
    return bestp,bestM
```
Need compute b1**t every iter can underflow to 0 after 1000; ok but small cost. Could use `bias1=1.0`? Standard uses (1-b1**t). For t>1000, close to 1. We can just not bias-correct after t>1000 to save; or keep arrays. Not critical.

But evaluating c only at top means after q update we don't know if M blew up until next loop; that's one iteration delay. Adaptive lr can use previous M stored after p update? We can compute c_new at end? We already computed c before updating; we can compute M_new after softmax with second conv? That doubles. But adaptive important. Maybe compute cheap upper bound? Not. We'll accept.

Top-k / simplex stage may be more direct. Let's implement `run_simplex_optimizer` as an alternative phase after log or before. It will be used to polish max with non-smooth top-k.

```
def run_simplex_optimizer(p0, time_limit, rng, beta_factor=10.0, k_factor=1.0):
    p = normalize; best...
    step=0.5? # multiplier for 1/g
    no_improve=0
    start...
    while time:
       c=conv(p); M=...
       update; gap=M-1/L
       beta = min(100*n, max(2*n, 10/gap)) ?
       progress=...
       k=int(4 + 80*progress)
       # weights mixture:
       lse_w = softmax beta
       top_w = zeros; idx = argpartition c [-k:]
       top_w[idx] = 1; normalize
       w = 0.5*top_w+0.5*lse_w; normalize
       gp=2*_corr(w,p)
       gnorm=norm(gp); if gnorm small break
       step_base = step / gnorm  # step initially 0.1? Let's set `use_step = step_base`
       # Try 3 steps: 0.5*step_base, 0.25, 0.1? Actually if step too large regress.
       accepted=False
       trial_steps=[step_base, 0.5*step_base, 0.25*step_base, 0.125*step_base]
       # To limit conv, only try until accepted. Evaluate c2 and surrogate? Use actual M.
       for st in trial_steps:
          p2=project_simplex(p - st*gp)
          c2=conv(p2); M2=c2.max()
          if M2 <= M * (1 - 1e-12) or (M2 < M and surrogate? )?
             # accept if M improves, else accept if surrogate improves? For progress, accept if M2 < best? But many tiny non-improvements could stagnate. Use accept if M2 < M - 1e-15 or surrogate2 < surrogate - 1e-18, but track best only on M. Maybe accept surrogate to move.
             if M2 < M - 1e-15 or (np.dot(w,c2) < np.dot(w,c) - 1e-18):
                p=p2; accepted=True; if M2<M: step=min(step*1.1, 2.0); else step*=0.8; break
          # else continue smaller
       if not accepted:
          # small random step? project tiny
          p2=project_simplex(p - (0.01/gnorm)*gp); p=p2 if objective not huge else revert
          no_improve++
          if no_improve>500:
             noise=0.01/np.sqrt(n)*rng.normal(0,1,n); p=project_simplex(p+noise); no_improve=0; step=0.1
       print occasionally
    return bestp,best
```
Projection and conv per trial heavy. Use fewer trials: try full, if not improve try half. Good.

Projection simple:
```
def _project_simplex(v, s=1.0):
    n=len(v)
    v=np.asarray(v,float).copy()
    if s<=0: return np.zeros(n)
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.nonzero(u > (css - s)/np.arange(1,n+1, dtype=float))[0]
    if len(rho)==0: return np.zeros(n)
    r=rho[-1]
    theta=(css[r]-s)/(r+1)
    w=v-theta
    w[w<0]=0
    return w
```
This ensures sum exactly? Floating maybe close. Normalize after projecting: `w/=w.sum()` to be safe.

For simplex step, after projection, sum can deviate; normalize.

`_normalize` if sum small: return uniform.

```
def _generate_powerlaw_grid(n, rng, n_samples=200):
   best=None; bestobj=inf
   for a in np.linspace(-1.5,1.5,11):
       for b in np.linspace(-1.5,1.5,11):
          x=np.arange(n)+1.0
          y=np.arange(n-1,-1,-1)+1.0
          p=x**a * y**b
          p=np.maximum(p,0); s=p.sum(); if s==0 continue; p/=s
          obj=_objective_value(p)
          if obj<best: best=p
   return best
```
This is only power law endpoints; may not be best. Add trig random:
```
def _generate_random_smooth(n, rng, n_samples=200, max_modes=8):
   best=None; bestobj=inf
   t=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
   for _ in range(n_samples):
       q=np.zeros(n)
       # linear/quadratic
       coef_lin = rng.uniform(-1,1)
       coef_quad = rng.uniform(-1,1)
       q += coef_lin*(t-0.5)
       q += coef_quad*(t-0.5)**2
       for m in range(1, max_modes+1):
           amp = rng.uniform(0, 1.0/(m*0.5))
           ph = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
           q += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*t + ph)
       # maybe sharpness scale
       p=np.exp(np.clip(2.0*q, -50,50))
       s=p.sum(); if s==0 continue
       p/=s
       obj=_objective_value(p)
       update
   return best
```

Let's start drafting code mentally.

```
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft

def _normalize(a, s=1.0): ...
def _uniform(n): return np.full(n, 1.0/n)

def _conv(a, N=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=len(a)
    L=2*n-1
    if N is None: N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    A=rfft(a, N)
    c=irfft(A*A, N).real
    c=c[:L]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c, N

def _objective(a):
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-12: return inf
    p=a/s
    c,N=_conv(p)
    return float(2.0*len(p)*c.max())

def _corr(a_b, p, N): # w,p
    n=len(p)
    W=rfft(a_b,N); P=rfft(p,N)
    corr=irfft(np.conj(P)*W, N).real[:n]
    return 2.0*corr
```
But in `_corr`, if N not computed from p and w? Use N from convolution. w length L maybe, N same. Need ensure w has length L. If w shorter? We'll pass length L.

For `_conv` we can return c,N. Many callers use c. For `_objective`, use `_conv`.

`_softmax(q)`:
```
def _softmax(q):
```

```
def _generate_powerlaw_start(n, grid_points=9):
    best=None; bestobj=np.inf
    x = (np.arange(n) + 1e-6)/n  # 0 to <1
    y = (n - np.arange(n) + 1e-6)/n  # reversed? For i=0, y≈1; i=n-1,y≈1e-6/n
    left = np.maximum(np.arange(n)+1e-6, 1e-6)/n
    right = np.maximum(n - np.arange(n) + 1e-6, 1e-6)/n
    for a in np.linspace(-1.2,1.6,grid_points):
        for b in np.linspace(-1.2,1.6,grid_points):
            p=left**a * right**b
            p=np.maximum(p,0)
            s=p.sum()
            if s<=0: continue
            p/=s
            obj=_objective(p)
            if obj<bestobj: ...
    return best
```

```
def _convolve_self(a):
    n=len(a); N=_fft_size(n)
    A=rfft(a,N)
    c=irfft(A*A,N).real[:2*n-1]
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    return c
```

Now, implement weights functions that operate on c and return w.
```
def _lse_weights(c, n):
    L=len(c)
    M=c.max()
    mean=1.0/L
    gap=M-mean
    if gap <= 1e-18:
       beta=100.0*n
    else:
       beta=min(100.0*n, 10.0/gap)
    scaled=beta*(c-M)
    scaled=np.clip(scaled, -60.0, 0.0)
    e=np.exp(scaled)
    e_sum=e.sum()
    if e_sum <=0 or not finite: e = np.zeros_like(c); e[np.argmax(c)] = 1.0
    w=e/e_sum
    # mix uniform
    w = 0.05*np.ones(L)/L + 0.95*w
    w /= w.sum()
    return w
```
For simplex mixture topk, maybe topk needs its own beta? We can use same LSE weights. Then:
```
def _active_topk_weights(c,k):
    L=len(c); k=min(k,L-1 if L>1 else 1)
    w=np.zeros(L)
    if k <=0: w[0]=1
    else:
       idx=np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
       w[idx]=1.0
       # maybe weight by c
       # normalize
    s=w.sum()
    return w/s
```
If k small and n large. Use if L.

```
def optimize_starts(candidates, time_budget, rng, verbose=True):
    if not candidates: return None, np.inf
    time_budget = max(1.0, time_budget)
    start0=time.monotonic()
    # candidates: list of (p,obj)
    # sort already? sort by obj
    candidates = sorted(candidates, key=lambda x: x[1]) # no lambda? Prompt no lambda functions. Need avoid lambda. Use a helper top-level `_candidate_obj(cand)` and sorted key? That is lambda? Could use `sorted(candidates, key=_obj_key)`. Define top-level `_obj_key(item): return item[1]`. Good.
    # limit 4
    max_cands=4
    shares=[0.5,0.3,0.15,0.05]
    bestp=None; bestobj=inf
    elapsed=0? Use absolute start.
    for i in range(min(max_cands,len(candidates))):
        if i>=len(shares): break
        p0,obj0=candidates[i]
        alloc_share=shares[i]
        t_est=alloc_share*time_budget
        remaining=time_budget - (time.monotonic()-start0)
        t_i=min(t_est, remaining)
        if t_i < 5: break
        if verbose: print(f"   start {i}: init_obj={obj0:.6f}, alloc={t_i:.0f}")
        p1,m1=run_mixed(p0,t_i,rng, verbose=verbose)
        if m1<bestobj: bestp=p1; bestobj=m1
        else: if bestp is None: bestp=p0; bestobj=obj0
        if time.monotonic()-start0 >= time_budget: break
    if bestp is None: bestp=candidates[0][0]; bestobj=candidates[0][1]
    return bestp,bestobj
```

Pseudo code details:

```
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft

def _fft_size(n):
    L=2*n-1
    return 1 << (L-1).bit_length()

def _normalize(a, s=1.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,None)
    ss=float(np.sum(a))
    if ss <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(len(a), dtype=np.float64)/max(1,len(a))
    return a*(s/ss)
```
If len(a)=0, return np.ones(1)? But sequence nonempty. In candidates, n positive. For safety if n=0 return empty? Use if a.size==0 return np.ones(1). But objective length 1 obj2. We'll avoid.

`_uniform(n): return np.full(n, 1.0/n)`

`_convolve_self(a)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float)
n=a.size
N=_fft_size(n)
A=rfft(a,N)
c=irfft(A*A, N).real
c=c[:2*n-1]
c = np.maximum(c,0.0)
return c
```
No dtype for A*A? ok.

`_objective_value(a)`:
```
s=float(a.sum())
if s <=0: return np.inf
p=a/s
c=_convolve_self(p)
return float(2.0*a.size*c.max())
```
If c.max nan? if p valid no.

`_softmax(q)`:
```
q=np.asarray(q,float)
mx=q.max()
if not np.isfinite(mx): return np.ones(q.size)/q.size
q=q-mx
q=np.clip(q, -700.0, 0.0)
p=np.exp(q)
ss=p.sum()
if ss<=0: return np.ones(q.size)/q.size
return p/ss
```

`_project_simplex(v,s=1)`:
```
n=len(v)
if n==0: return np.array([],float)
v=np.asarray(v,float).copy()
if s<=0: return np.zeros(n)
u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
cum=np.cumsum(u)
idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=np.float64)
cond = u * idx > (cum - s)? condition tc <= w: (cum-s)/idx <= u -> cum - s <= u*idx. Due to floating. Use valid = (cum - s) <= u*idx
if not any: return np.full(n,s/n)
rho = int(np.max(np.where(valid)[0])) +1
theta = (cum[rho-1]-s)/rho
w = v - theta
w[w<0]=0
ss=w.sum()
if ss<=0: return np.full(n,s/n)
return w*(s/ss)
```
Need valid array same length; if no valid, uniform.

`_interpolate(old,n)`:
```
old=np.asarray(old,float).reshape(-1); old=np.clip(old,0,None)
if old.size<2: return _uniform(n) if old.size==0 else np.full(n, old[0])? Actually if size1 and normalize sum1? We'll call normalize later.
xo=np.linspace(0,1,old.size)
xn=np.linspace(0,1,n)
new=np.interp(xn,xo,old)
return _normalize(new)
```

`_symmetrize(p)`:
```
p=np.asarray(p,float)
return _normalize(0.5*(p+p[::-1]))
```

`_candidate_sort_key(c): return c[1]`

```
left = np.arange(n, dtype=float) + 1.0  # maybe 1..n

# To allow endpoint zero-ish, use distances 1..n; for exponents positive, endpoints minimal 1^n? Actually left[0]=1, right[n-1]=1, so no zero. Use distances from 0: np.arange(n) and n-1-arange, + eps.
left0 = np.arange(n, dtype=float) + 1e-6
right0 = (n-1 - np.arange(n, dtype=float)) + 1e-6
# Normalize distances by n for scale? exponents apply to distances; scale not important.
logl=np.log(left0); logr=np.log(right0)
for a in ...:
   p=np.exp(a*logl + b*logr)
```
If a negative large, p[0]=exp(-2*6e-6?) ~1, p small at right. Good. If a=1.6, p[0]=eps^1.6 ~1e-10, endpoint zero-ish. Good.
Use `np.exp` may overflow if a*log ~1.6*log(32767)~22, ok. For negative -1.2*6e-6=-7e-6. fine. Normalize.
Grid exponents: from -1.5 to 2.0? Endpoint positive exponent can zero endpoints. Let's use 9 points: `np.linspace(-1.0, 2.0, 9)` for both. Maybe a,b >1 endpoints too small. ok.

`_generate_random_smooth_start(n, rng, trials=70, modes=5)`:
Precompute t=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n once.
For each:
```
q = np.zeros(n)
amp = rng.uniform(0.3, 1.5)
q += rng.uniform(-0.8,0.8)*amp*(t-0.5)
q += rng.uniform(-0.8,0.8)*amp*((t-0.5)**2 - 1.111/11?) # variance
# Add a bump
if rng.random()<0.8:
   center=rng.uniform(0.05,0.95); width=rng.uniform(0.05,0.5); height=rng.uniform(-1,1)*amp
   q += height * np.exp(-0.5*((t-center)/width)**2)
for m in range(1,modes+1):
   amp_m = amp / (m*0.8)
   phase = rng.uniform(-np.pi,np.pi)
   q += rng.uniform(-amp_m, amp_m)*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*t+phase)
   q += rng.uniform(-amp_m, amp_m)*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*t+phase)
p=np.exp(np.clip(1.5*q, -60,60)); p/=p.sum()
obj
```
This may generate high oscillations; full optimizer can smooth. Need maybe not too bad. We can also plateau.

`_build_starts(n, base_p, rng, max_starts=4)`:
```
cands=[]
base=None
if base_p is not None and len(base_p)>0:
    base=_normalize(base_p)
# interp
if base is not None:
    p=_interpolate(base,n)
    cands.append((p, _objective_value(p)))
    p=_symmetrize(p)
    cands.append((p, _objective_value(p)))
    # perturbations
    q=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-12)); q-=q.mean()
    for sc in (0.05,0.2):
       p2=_softmax(q + sc*rng.normal(0,1,n))
       cands.append((p2, _objective_value(p2)))
# uniform? append if cands empty? 
# power
p=_generate_powerlaw_start(n)
if p is not None: cands.append((p,_objective_value(p)))
# random
p=_generate_random_smooth_start(n,rng)
if p is not None: cands.append((p,_objective_value(p)))
# if none, uniform
if not cands:
   p=_uniform(n); cands.append((p,_objective_value(p)))
# filter finite and sort; remove duplicate objectives
cands = [c for c in cands if np.isfinite(c[1])]
cands.sort(key=_candidate_sort_key)
# Deduplicate similar: keep first; for rest skip if obj > cands[0][1] + 0.02? Maybe no.
# Limit max_starts
return cands[:max_starts]
```
Need no nested. This function calls objective for each candidate, which for n 32768 and 6 candidates ~6 conv, ok. Power/random internally call many convs.

Time in `_build_starts` power for 32k 81 conv, random 70 conv. Could be ~1-2s. ok.

But for provided phase n=8192, also. Total maybe ok.

`_optimize_starts(candidates, time_budget, rng, verbose=True)` as above. Need no closures. It uses `_run_mixed`.

`_run_mixed(p0, time_budget, rng, verbose=True)`:
```
p0=_normalize(p0); n=len(p0)
bestp=p0.copy(); bestobj=_objective_value(p0)
start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+time_budget
# stage allocations relative to time_budget
t1=min(time_budget*0.30, max(0, deadline-time.monotonic()))
if t1>5:
    p1,m1=_run_simplex(p0, t1, rng, verbose=verbose)
    if m1<bestobj: bestp,bestobj=p1,m1
# stage2 log 35
remaining=deadline-time.monotonic(); t2=min(time_budget*0.35, remaining)
if t2>5: p2,m2=_run_log(bestp,t2,rng,verbose=...); update
# stage3 simplex 15
rem=...; t3=min(time_budget*0.15, rem); if t3>5: run_simplex(bestp...)
# stage4 log remaining
rem=deadline-time.monotonic(); if rem>5: run_log(bestp, rem,...)
return bestp,bestobj
```
For random starts, simplex first maybe if p values positive. ok.

Maybe if objective initial very high (>2.5), use log first? We can decide:
```
if obj0 > 2.2: stage1 log 0.4, stage2 simplex 0.3, ... else simplex first.
```

```
   order = (log 0.4, simplex 0.3, log 0.2, simplex 0.1)

```

`_run_simplex(p0, time_limit, rng, verbose=True)`, `_run_log` similar.

Let's write `_run_simplex` carefully:
```
def _run_simplex(p0, time_limit, rng, verbose=True):
    p=_normalize(p0); n=p.size; L=2*n-1; N=_fft_size(n); mean=1.0/L
    c=_convolve_self(p); M=float(c.max())
    bestp=p.copy(); bestM=M
    step_mult=0.05
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+time_limit
    last_improve=start; last_print=start
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it +=1
       # assume p,c,M current; but after update we set. At top we can use current c? We need c from p. At start ok; after update at end we compute c for p_new and set. So at top no recompute.
       # weights
       lse_w = _lse_weights(c,n)
       k = int(4 + 80.0 * min(1.0, it/max(1,int(time_limit*10))? better progress time)))
       progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-start)/max(1e-6,time_limit))
       k = int(4 + 80*progress)
       top_w = _topk_weights(c,k)
       w = 0.5*top_w + 0.5*lse_w
       # normalize again
       ws=w.sum(); if ws<=0: w=top_w; else w/=ws
       P = rfft(p,N)
       W = rfft(w,N)
       corr = irfft(np.conj(P)*W, N).real[:n]
       gp = 2.0*corr
       gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(gp))
       if gnorm < 1e-15: break
       sur = float(np.dot(w, c))
       base = step_mult / gnorm
       factors = (1.0, 0.5, 0.25)
       accepted = False
       M_old = M
       for f in factors:
           st = base*f
           p_try = _project_simplex(p - st*gp, 1.0)
           c_try = _convolve_self(p_try)
           M_try = float(c_try.max())
           sur_try = float(np.dot(w, c_try))
           if M_try < M_old - 1e-15 or sur_try < sur - 1e-16:
               # potential objective improvement?
               p = p_try; c = c_try; M = M_try; accepted=True
               if M_try < M_old - 1e-15:
                   step_mult = min(0.3, step_mult*1.05)
                   if M_try < bestM - 1e-15*max(1.0,bestM):
                       bestM=M_try; bestp=p.copy(); last_improve=time.monotonic()
                   if verbose and time.monotonic()-last_print>20: ...
               else:
                   step_mult = min(0.3, step_mult*1.02)
               break
       if not accepted:
           step_mult = max(0.002, step_mult*0.6)
           if time.monotonic()-last_improve > 40.0:
               noise = (0.05 / n) * rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
               p = _project_simplex(p + noise, 1.0)
               c = _convolve_self(p); M=float(c.max())
               step_mult=0.05
               last_improve=time.monotonic()
       # if accepted but M increased, check if M too high? If M > bestM*1.5 maybe revert? But be careful. Could use if M > 2*bestM: p=bestp; c=...; M=bestM; step_mult*=0.5
       if M > 2.0*bestM and bestM < np.inf:
           # Too unstable; revert to best
           p=bestp.copy(); M=bestM; c=_convolve_self(p); step_mult=max(0.002,step_mult*0.5)
```
This recomputes c after update in line search; then top of loop uses current c. Good. But on `not accepted` without try? We still computed up to 3 conv in factors. If all fail, p unchanged, c unchanged, M_old. Then noise maybe. good.

But if accepted by surrogate but M increased significantly, c/M set to worse. We have `if M > 2*bestM revert` after. Good.

Potential inefficiency: In factors loop, if full fails but half succeeds, we computed full and half; okay. If full succeeds only surrogate, M may increase. Good.

Stagnation: Time-based. `last_improve` updates only when bestM improves. But if M improves not best? best is min so any M improvement updates best. So okay. If objective plateaus, noise after 40s. Good.

Need `_topk_weights(c,k)`:
```
def _topk_weights(c,k):
    L=len(c); k=int(k)
    if k<=0:
       w=np.zeros(L); w[np.argmax(c)]=1; return w
    if k>=L:
       return np.ones(L)/L
    idx=np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
    w=np.zeros(L)
    w[idx]=1.0
    s=w.sum()
    if s<=0: w[np.argmax(c)]=1
    else: w/=s
    return w
```
If k=1, `-k` = -1 works? `np.argpartition(c, -1)` returns one. ok. If L=1, k>=L returns ones.

`_lse_weights(c,n)`:
Need `mean` maybe from sum(c) not exact 1/L; use `mean=float(c.sum())/L` for safety. Since c sum may deviate. Use `mean = c.mean()`.
```
def _lse_weights(c,n):
    M=float(c.max())
    mean=float(np.mean(c))
    gap=M-mean
    beta_cap=80.0*n
    if gap <= 1e-18:
        beta=beta_cap
    else:
        beta=min(beta_cap, 10.0/gap)
    scaled=beta*(c-M)
    scaled=np.clip(scaled,-60.0,0.0)
    e=np.exp(scaled); s=e.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite:
       w=np.zeros_like(c); imax=int(np.argmax(c)); w[imax]=1
    else: w=e/s
    uniform = np.full_like(c, 1.0/L)
    w = 0.05*uniform + 0.95*w
    s=w.sum(); if s>0: w/=s
    return w
```
Use L=len.

In simplex, top_w and lse_w; if k grows, top_w broad; maybe mixture too broad. Fine.

Now `_run_log`:
```
def _run_log(p0, time_limit, rng, verbose=True):
    p=_normalize(p0); n=p.size; N=_fft_size(n); L=2*n-1
    c=_convolve_self(p); M=float(c.max()); bestM=M; bestp=p.copy()
    q=np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-15)); q-=q.mean(); np.clip(q,-60,60,out=q)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=1; lr=0.02; 
    b1=0.9; b2=0.999; eps=1e-8
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+time_limit; last_improve=start; last_print=start
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it+=1
       P=rfft(p,N)
       c=irfft(P*P,N).real[:L]; c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
       M=float(c.max())
       if M < bestM - 1e-15*max(1.0,bestM):
           bestM=M; bestp=p.copy(); last_improve=time.monotonic(); lr=min(0.05, lr*1.03)
       if verbose and it... print
       if M > 5.0*bestM:
           q=np.log(np.maximum(bestp,1e-15)); q-=q.mean(); np.clip...
           m.fill(0); v.fill(0); t=1; lr=max(0.001,lr*0.5)
           p=_softmax(q); continue
       w=_lse_weights(c,n)
       W=rfft(w,N)
       corr=irfft(np.conj(P)*W,N).real[:n]
       gp=2.0*corr
       gp_dot=float(np.dot(p,gp))
       gq=p*(gp-gp_dot)
       gnorm=float(np.linalg.norm(gq))
       if gnorm < 1e-14: break
       # Adam update using t
       t += 1
       m = (1-b1)*m + b1*gq
       v = (1-b2)*v + b2*gq*gq
       # avoid recomputing powers? Use precomputed bias? We'll compute r=1-b1**t; for t large powers 0. Could maintain:
       # `correction1 = 1.0 - (b1 ** t)`; b1**t underflows quickly; cost small but each iter. We can just use 1.0 after t>1000.
       if t < 1000:
           mhat = m/(1.0 - b1**t)
           vhat = v/(1.0 - b2**t)
       else:
           mhat = m; vhat = v
       update = lr * mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+eps)
       q = q - update
       # decay and center
       q *= 0.999999
       q -= q.mean()
       np.clip(q,-60.0,60.0,out=q)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(q)):
           q=np.log(np.maximum(bestp,1e-15)); q-=q.mean(); fill...
       p=_softmax(q)
       if time.monotonic() - last_improve > 45.0:
           # noise
           q = q + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, n)
           q -= q.mean(); np.clip(...)
           p=_softmax(q)
           lr = max(0.002, lr*0.8)
           last_improve = time.monotonic()
    return bestp,bestM
```
Need ensure when large t, mhat m may have scale? Bias correction not needed. `vhat` may be zero for coordinates no gradient; eps handles. `lr` in q units. q update may be big if mhat/sqrt(vhat) ~1 but coordinate-specific; lr 0.02. Good.

But if gq all zeros, gnorm break. If flat optimum, break.

Potential issue: `lr *= 1.03` on every best improvement could increase too fast; cap 0.05. If plateau but small improvements continue, lr max. ok.

Noise scale 0.05 in q small; if stuck long, maybe insufficient. We can make noise increase: keep `noise_scale=0.05`; if >120s no improve, noise_scale=0.2. Simpler:
```
stuck_time = time - last_improve
noise = 0.02 + 0.02*min(5, stuck_time/60)
```
Ok.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2.

The main program keeps probabilities p = a / sum(a) and minimizes
    2*n*max(p*p).

It combines:
  - multi-length exploration (8192, 16384, 32768),
  - diverse smooth starts from the previous construction, power laws, and
    random Fourier/bump initializations,
  - projected subgradient descent on the simplex with top-k/LSE weights,
  - log-domain Adam on a smooth log-sum-exp surrogate,
  - periodic perturbations and final polishing.

All heavy convolution work is done with FFT. The final candidate uses the
best sequence found according to the internal objective and is validated once
with the provided evaluate_sequence.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft


def _fft_size(n):
    """Next power of two large enough for full linear self-convolution."""
    L = 2 * n - 1
    return 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()


def _uniform(n):
    return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)


def _normalize(a, s=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    ss = float(np.sum(a))
    if ss <= 1e-300 or a.size == 0:
        return _uniform(max(1, a.size))
    a = a * (s / ss)
    return a


def _softmax(q):
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    mx = float(np.max(q))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return _uniform(q.size)
    q = q - mx
    q = np.clip(q, -650.0, 0.0)
    p = np.exp(q)
    ss = float(np.sum(p))
    if ss <= 1e-300:
        return _uniform(q.size)
    return p / ss


def _project_simplex(v, s=1.0):
    """Euclidean projection onto {x >= 0, sum(x)=s}."""
    n = len(v)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if s <= 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cum = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    valid = (cum - s) <= (u * idx)
    if not np.any(valid):
        return np.full(n, s / float(n), dtype=np.float64)

    rho = int(np.max(np.where(valid)[0])) + 1
    theta = (cum[rho - 1] - s) / float(rho)
    w = v - theta
    w[w < 0.0] = 0.0
    ss = float(np.sum(w))
    if ss <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n, s / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    return w * (s / ss)


def _convolve_self(a):
    """Full linear self-convolution via FFT, clipped to non-negative."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    N = _fft_size(n)
    A = rfft(a, N)
    c = irfft(A * A, N).real
    c = c[:2 * n - 1]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _objective_value(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    ss = float(np.sum(a))
    if a.size == 0 or ss <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    p = a / ss
    c = _convolve_self(p)
    return float(2.0 * float(a.size) * float(np.max(c)))


def _interpolate(old, n):
    old = np.asarray(old, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    old = np.clip(old, 0.0, None)
    if old.size == 0:
        return _uniform(n)
    if old.size == 1:
        return _uniform(n)
    xo = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, old.size, dtype=np.float64)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.interp(xn, xo, old)
    return _normalize(y)


def _symmetrize(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    return _normalize(0.5 * (p + p[::-1]))


def _candidate_sort_key(item):
    return item[1]


def _topk_weights(c, k):
    L = len(c)
    k = int(k)
    if L == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if k <= 0:
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    if k >= L:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / float(L), dtype=np.float64)

    idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    w[idx] = 1.0
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if s <= 0.0:
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= s
    return w


def _lse_weights(c, n):
    L = len(c)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    mean = float(np.mean(c))
    gap = M - mean
    beta_cap = 80.0 * float(n)
    if gap <= 1e-18:
        beta = beta_cap
    else:
        beta = min(beta_cap, max(2.0 * float(n), 10.0 / gap))

    scaled = beta * (c - M)
    scaled = np.clip(scaled, -60.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(scaled)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w = e / s

    # Small uniform floor keeps the smooth surrogate globally balanced.
    w *= 0.95
    w += 0.05 / float(L)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if s <= 0.0:
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= s
    return w


def _generate_powerlaw_start(n, grid=9):
    """Best power-law endpoint shape over a coarse grid of exponents."""
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    left = i + 1e-6
    right = (n - 1) - i + 1e-6
    logl = np.log(left)
    logr = np.log(right)

    exponents = np.linspace(-1.0, 2.0, int(grid))
    best_p = None
    best_obj = np.inf

    for a in exponents:
        for b in exponents:
            q = a * logl + b * logr
            p = np.exp(np.clip(q, -600.0, 600.0))
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
            s = float(np.sum(p))
            if s <= 1e-300:
                continue
            p /= s
            obj = _objective_value(p)
            if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_obj:
                best_obj = obj
                best_p = p.copy()
    return best_p


def _generate_random_smooth_start(n, rng, trials=55, modes=4):
    """A random smooth initial probability vector, evaluated directly."""
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    t = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    best_p = None
    best_obj = np.inf

    for _ in range(int(trials)):
        q = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.35, 1.6))
        q += float(rng.uniform(-0.9, 0.9)) * amp * (t - 0.5)
        q += float(rng.uniform(-0.9, 0.9)) * amp * ((t - 0.5) ** 2)

        if float(rng.random()) < 0.85:
            center = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.95))
            width = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.45))
            height = float(rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0)) * amp
            q += height * np.exp(-0.5 * ((t - center) / width) ** 2)

        for m in range(1, int(modes) + 1):
            a1 = float(rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0)) * amp / (0.6 + 0.7 * m)
            ph1 = float(rng.uniform(-np.pi, np.pi))
            q += a1 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph1)
            a2 = float(rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0)) * amp / (0.6 + 0.7 * m)
            ph2 = float(rng.uniform(-np.pi, np.pi))
            q += a2 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph2)

        p = np.exp(np.clip(1.6 * q, -650.0, 650.0))
        p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
        s = float(np.sum(p))
        if s <= 1e-300:
            continue
        p /= s
        obj = _objective_value(p)
        if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_obj:
            best_obj = obj
            best_p = p.copy()

    return best_p


def _build_starts(n, base_p, rng, max_starts=4):
    """Build a sorted list of diversity / quality candidate starts."""
    cands = []
    base = None
    if base_p is not None and len(base_p) > 0:
        base = _normalize(base_p)

    if base is not None:
        p0 = _interpolate(base, n)
        obj = _objective_value(p0)
        if np.isfinite(obj):
            cands.append((p0, obj))

        for alpha in (0.20, 0.50, 0.80):
            p = _normalize(alpha * p0 + (1.0 - alpha) * p0[::-1])
            obj = _objective_value(p)
            if np.isfinite(obj):
                cands.append((p, obj))

        q0 = np.log(np.maximum(p0, 1e-12))
        q0 -= float(np.mean(q0))
        for scale in (0.05, 0.18):
            p = _softmax(q0 + scale * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n))
            obj = _objective_value(p)
            if np.isfinite(obj):
                cands.append((p, obj))

    pp = _generate_powerlaw_start(n)
    if pp is not None:
        obj = _objective_value(pp)
        if np.isfinite(obj):
            cands.append((pp, obj))

    pr = _generate_random_smooth_start(n, rng)
    if pr is not None:
        obj = _objective_value(pr)
        if np.isfinite(obj):
            cands.append((pr, obj))

    if not cands:
        pu = _uniform(n)
        cands.append((pu, _objective_value(pu)))

    cands = [x for x in cands if np.isfinite(x[1])]
    cands.sort(key=_candidate_sort_key)
    return cands[:int(max_starts)]


def _run_simplex(p0, time_limit, rng, verbose=True):
    """Projected subgradient / line-search stage on the simplex."""
    p = _normalize(p0)
    n = p.size
    N = _fft_size(n)

    c = _convolve_self(p)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_M = M

    step_mult = 0.05
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + float(time_limit)
    last_improve = start
    last_print = start
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - start) / max(1e-12, float(time_limit)))

        lse_w = _lse_weights(c, n)
        k = int(4 + 120.0 * progress)
        top_w = _topk_weights(c, k)
        w = 0.5 * top_w + 0.5 * lse_w
        ws = float(np.sum(w))
        if ws <= 0.0:
            w = top_w
        else:
            w /= ws

        P = rfft(p, N)
        W = rfft(w, N)
        corr = irfft(np.conj(P) * W, N).real[:n]
        grad = 2.0 * corr
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
        if gnorm < 1e-15:
            break

        sur = float(np.dot(w, c))
        base_step = step_mult / gnorm
        accepted = False
        old_M = M

        for factor in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25):
            st = base_step * factor
            p_try = _project_simplex(p - st * grad, 1.0)
            c_try = _convolve_self(p_try)
            M_try = float(np.max(c_try))
            sur_try = float(np.dot(w, c_try))

            accept = False
            if M_try + 1e-15 * max(1.0, old_M) < old_M:
                accept = True
            if sur_try + 1e-15 * max(1.0, sur) < sur:
                accept = True

            if accept:
                p = p_try
                c = c_try
                M = M_try
                accepted = True

                if M_try + 1e-15 * max(1.0, old_M) < old_M:
                    step_mult = min(0.30, step_mult * 1.05)
                    if M_try < best_M:
                        best_M = M_try
                        best_p = p.copy()
                        last_improve = time.monotonic()
                else:
                    step_mult = min(0.30, step_mult * 1.02)
                break

        if not accepted:
            step_mult = max(0.002, step_mult * 0.6)
            if time.monotonic() - last_improve > 40.0:
                noise = (0.05 / float(n)) * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
                p = _project_simplex(p + noise, 1.0)
                c = _convolve_self(p)
                M = float(np.max(c))
                step_mult = 0.05
                last_improve = time.monotonic()

        # If we wandered too far, snap back to the local best.
        if M > 2.0 * best_M:
            p = best_p.copy()
            M = best_M
            c = _convolve_self(p)
            step_mult = max(0.002, step_mult * 0.5)

        if verbose and it % 700 == 0 and time.monotonic() - last_print > 20.0:
            last_print = time.monotonic()
            print(f"      simplex  n={n} obj={2.0 * float(n) * best_M:.7f} "
                  f"t={time.monotonic() - start:.0f}s", flush=True)

    return best_p, best_M


def _run_log_lse(p0, time_limit, rng, verbose=True):
    """Log-domain Adam stage minimizing an LSE surrogate."""
    p = _normalize(p0)
    n = p.size
    N = _fft_size(n)
    L = 2 * n - 1

    c = _convolve_self(p)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_M = M

    q = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-15))
    q -= float(np.mean(q))
    q = np.clip(q, -60.0, 60.0)

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b1 = 0.90
    b2 = 0.999
    eps = 1e-8
    t = 1
    lr = 0.02

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + float(time_limit)
    last_improve = start
    last_print = start
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        P = rfft(p, N)
        c = irfft(P * P, N).real[:L]
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        M = float(np.max(c))

        if M < best_M:
            best_M = M
            best_p = p.copy()
            last_improve = time.monotonic()
            lr = min(0.05, lr * 1.03)

        # Catastrophic divergence guard.
        if M > 5.0 * best_M:
            q = np.log(np.maximum(best_p, 1e-15))
            q -= float(np.mean(q))
            q = np.clip(q, -60.0, 60.0)
            m.fill(0.0)
            v.fill(0.0)
            t = 1
            lr = max(0.001, lr * 0.5)
            p = _softmax(q)
            continue

        w = _lse_weights(c, n)
        W = rfft(w, N)
        corr = irfft(np.conj(P) * W, N).real[:n]
        gp = 2.0 * corr

        # Exact gradient of F(softmax(q)) for F = sum w_k c_k.
        dot_pg = float(np.dot(p, gp))
        gq = p * (gp - dot_pg)
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(gq))
        if gnorm < 1e-14:
            break

        t += 1
        m = (1.0 - b1) * m + b1 * gq
        v = (1.0 - b2) * v + b2 * (gq * gq)

        if t < 1000:
            mhat = m / (1.0 - b1 ** t)
            vhat = v / (1.0 - b2 ** t)
        else:
            mhat = m
            vhat = v

        delta = lr * mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + eps)
        q = q - delta

        # Gentle shrinkage keeps q from wandering to unsupported extremes.
        q *= 0.999999
        q -= float(np.mean(q))
        q = np.clip(q, -60.0, 60.0)

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(q)):
            q = np.log(np.maximum(best_p, 1e-15))
            q -= float(np.mean(q))
            q = np.clip(q, -60.0, 60.0)
            m.fill(0.0)
            v.fill(0.0)
            t = 1
            lr = max(0.001, lr * 0.5)

        p = _softmax(q)

        stuck = time.monotonic() - last_improve
        if stuck > 45.0:
            noise_scale = 0.02 + 0.02 * min(4.0, stuck / 60.0)
            q = q + rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, n)
            q -= float(np.mean(q))
            q = np.clip(q, -60.0, 60.0)
            p = _softmax(q)
            lr = max(0.002, lr * 0.8)
            last_improve = time.monotonic()

        if verbose and it % 700 == 0 and time.monotonic() - last_print > 20.0:
            last_print = time.monotonic()
            print(f"      log-lse  n={n} obj={2.0 * float(n) * best_M:.7f} "
                  f"t={time.monotonic() - start:.0f}s", flush=True)

    return best_p, best_M


def _run_mixed(p0, time_budget, rng, verbose=True):
    """Run simplex/log stages in a balanced sequence."""
    p0 = _normalize(p0)
    n = p0.size
    init_c = _convolve_self(p0)
    best_M = float(np.max(init_c))
    best_p = p0.copy()

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + float(time_budget)

    obj0 = 2.0 * float(n) * best_M
    if obj0 > 2.5:
        stages = (
            ("log", 0.35),
            ("simplex", 0.30),
            ("log", 0.20),
            ("simplex", 0.15),
        )
    else:
        stages = (
            ("simplex", 0.30),
            ("log", 0.35),
            ("simplex", 0.15),
            ("log", 0.20),
        )

    for kind, frac in stages:
        remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
        alloc = min(float(time_budget) * float(frac), remaining)
        if alloc < 5.0:
            continue

        if kind == "simplex":
            p_new, M_new = _run_simplex(best_p, alloc, rng, verbose=verbose)
        else:
            p_new, M_new = _run_log_lse(best_p, alloc, rng, verbose=verbose)

        if M_new < best_M:
            best_p = p_new.copy()
            best_M = float(M_new)

        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

    return best_p, best_M


def _optimize_starts(candidates, time_budget, rng, verbose=True):
    """Optimize the most promising starts, with a final/global polishing pass."""
    if not candidates:
        return None, np.inf

    n = len(candidates[0][0])
    time_budget = max(1.0, float(time_budget))
    start0 = time.monotonic()

    best_p = None
    best_M = np.inf

    shares = (0.40, 0.25, 0.10, 0.05)
    max_cands = min(len(candidates), len(shares))

    for i in range(int(max_cands)):
        p_init, obj_init = candidates[i]
        M_init = obj_init / (2.0 * float(n))
        if best_M is None or M_init < best_M:
            best_M = M_init
            best_p = _normalize(p_init)

        remaining = max(0.0, time_budget - (time.monotonic() - start0))
        alloc = min(time_budget * shares[i], remaining)
        if alloc < 5.0:
            break

        if verbose:
            print(f"    start {i + 1}/{max_cands}: init_obj={obj_init:.7f}, alloc={alloc:.0f}s",
                  flush=True)

        p_new, M_new = _run_mixed(best_p if i == 0 else p_init, alloc, rng, verbose=verbose)
        if M_new < best_M:
            best_M = float(M_new)
            best_p = p_new.copy()

        if time.monotonic() - start0 >= time_budget:
            break

    remaining = max(0.0, time_budget - (time.monotonic() - start0))
    if remaining > 10.0 and best_p is not None:
        if verbose:
            print(f"    global polish within this length: alloc={remaining:.0f}s", flush=True)
        p_new, M_new = _run_mixed(best_p, remaining, rng, verbose=verbose)
        if M_new < best_M:
            best_M = float(M_new)
            best_p = p_new.copy()

    if best_p is None:
        best_p = _normalize(candidates[0][0])
        best_M = candidates[0][1] / (2.0 * float(n))

    return best_p, best_M


def _phase_for_length(n, base_p, time_budget, rng, verbose=True):
    start = time.monotonic()
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return None, np.inf
    if time_budget <= 5.0:
        cands = _build_starts(n, base_p, rng, max_starts=1)
        if not cands:
            return None, np.inf
        p = _normalize(cands[0][0])
        M = cands[0][1] / (2.0 * float(n))
        return p, M

    cands = _build_starts(n, base_p, rng, max_starts=4)
    if not cands:
        return None, np.inf

    remaining = max(0.0, float(time_budget) - (time.monotonic() - start))
    if remaining <= 5.0:
        p = _normalize(cands[0][0])
        M = cands[0][1] / (2.0 * float(n))
        return p, M

    if verbose:
        best_init = min(obj for _, obj in cands)
        print(f"  phase n={n}: best_init_obj={best_init:.7f}, time={remaining:.0f}s", flush=True)

    return _optimize_starts(cands, remaining, rng, verbose=verbose)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """Return the best sequence found under the given time budget."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    t0 = time.monotonic()

    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    work_budget = max(60.0, total_budget - 120.0)
    deadline = t0 + work_budget

    print(f"Total budget={total_budget:.0f}s, work deadline leaves 120s for final validation.")
    print(f"Start time t=0.0s", flush=True)

    # Load previous construction, if present.
    prov = None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if arr.size >= 16 and float(np.sum(arr)) > 0.0:
            prov = _normalize(arr)
            print(f"Loaded height_sequence_1: len={arr.size}, internal obj={_objective_value(prov):.7f}")
    except Exception:
        prov = None

    best_p = None
    best_obj = np.inf
    best_by_len = {}

    # Choose length phases.
    if prov is None:
        phase_specs = [
            (16384, 0.58, None),
            (32768, 0.32, None),
        ]
    else:
        nprov = len(prov)
        if nprov == 8192:
            phase_specs = [
                (8192, 0.15, prov),
                (16384, 0.47, None),
                (32768, 0.28, None),
            ]
        elif nprov == 16384:
            phase_specs = [
                (16384, 0.30, prov),
                (32768, 0.48, None),
            ]
        elif nprov == 32768:
            phase_specs = [
                (32768, 0.68, prov),
                (16384, 0.18, None),
            ]
        else:
            phase_specs = []
            if 256 <= nprov <= 65536:
                phase_specs.append((nprov, 0.20, prov))
            if not any(abs(nprov - 16384) <= 4096 for nprov_x, _, _ in phase_specs):
                phase_specs.append((16384, 0.40, None))
            if not any(abs(nprov - 32768) <= 4096 for nprov_x, _, _ in phase_specs):
                phase_specs.append((32768, 0.30, None))
            if not phase_specs:
                phase_specs = [(16384, 0.60, None), (32768, 0.30, None)]

    for n_target, frac, base_choice in phase_specs:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30.0:
            break

        if n_target < 64 or n_target > 32768:
            continue

        alloc = min(work_budget * float(frac), max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic()))
        if alloc < 30.0:
            continue

        if base_choice is None:
            base_use = best_p if best_p is not None else prov
        else:
            base_use = base_choice

        try:
            p_new, M_new = _phase_for_length(n_target, base_use, alloc, rng, verbose=True)
        except Exception as exc:
            print(f"Phase n={n_target} failed: {exc}", flush=True)
            continue

        if p_new is None or not np.isfinite(M_new):
            continue

        obj_new = 2.0 * float(n_target) * float(M_new)
        if np.isfinite(obj_new):
            if obj_new < best_obj:
                best_obj = float(obj_new)
                best_p = _normalize(p_new).copy()
                best_by_len[n_target] = best_p.copy()
                print(f"  NEW GLOBAL BEST: len={n_target}, obj={best_obj:.7f}", flush=True)

    # Final global polishing.
    remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    if best_p is not None and remaining > 30.0:
        n_best = int(len(best_p))
        print(f"Final global polish: len={n_best}, time={remaining:.0f}s", flush=True)
        try:
            p_new, M_new = _run_mixed(best_p, remaining, rng, verbose=True)
            if p_new is not None and np.isfinite(M_new):
                obj_new = 2.0 * float(n_best) * float(M_new)
                if obj_new < best_obj:
                    best_obj = float(obj_new)
                    best_p = _normalize(p_new).copy()
                    best_by_len[n_best] = best_p.copy()
                    print(f"  NEW GLOBAL BEST: len={n_best}, obj={best_obj:.7f}", flush=True)
        except Exception as exc:
            print(f"Final polish failed: {exc}", flush=True)

    if best_p is None:
        print("Fallback to uniform sequence.", flush=True)
        best_p = _uniform(8192)
        best_obj = _objective_value(best_p)

    best_p = np.asarray(best_p, dtype=np.float64)
    best_p = np.clip(best_p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    ss = float(np.sum(best_p))
    if ss <= 0.01:
        best_p = _uniform(8192)
        ss = float(np.sum(best_p))
    best_p *= (1.0 / ss)

    final_list = [float(x) for x in best_p]
    n_final = len(final_list)
    print(f"Draft final list: len={n_final}, internal_obj={best_obj:.7f}", flush=True)

    try:
        verdict = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
        print(f"evaluate_sequence(final_list) = {float(verdict):.7f}", flush=True)
    except Exception as exc:
        verdict = np.inf
        print(f"evaluate_sequence failed: {exc}", flush=True)

    if not np.isfinite(verdict):
        # Conservative fallback to the provided sequence if available, else uniform.
        if prov is not None:
            final_list = [float(x) for x in np.clip(prov, 0.0, 1000.0)]
        else:
            final_list = [float(x) for x in _uniform(8192)]
        print("Using fallback final list.", flush=True)

    return final_list
```
